<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<issue_description>
Inconsequent ignoring
Let's take a look at this example:

```
% echo "py==1.11.0" | skjold audit -s pyup -s gemnasium -
Warning: No advisory sources configured!

py==1.11.0 (<=1.11.0) via gemnasium as CVE-2022-42969 found in <stdin>

Regular expression Denial of Service. The py library through 1.11.0 for Python
allows remote attackers to conduct a ReDoS (Regular expression Denial of
Service) attack via a Subversion repository with crafted info data, because the
InfoSvnCommand argument is mishandled.
https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2022-42969

https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2022-42969
https://pypi.org/project/py
https://github.com/pytest-dev/py/blob/cb87a83960523a2367d0f19226a73aed4ce4291d/py/_path/svnurl.py#L316
https://github.com/pytest-dev/py/issues/287
--

py==1.11.0 (<=1.11.0) via pyup as pyup.io-51457 found in <stdin>

Py throughout 1.11.0 allows remote attackers to conduct a ReDoS (Regular
expression Denial of Service) attack via a Subversion repository with crafted
info data, because the InfoSvnCommand argument is mishandled.
https://github.com/pytest-dev/py/issues/287
https://pyup.io/vulnerabilities/CVE-2022-42969/51457/

--
Found 1 vulnerable package(s)!
```

Both `pyup` and `gemnasium` finds this package as a vulnerability. However when using `pyup` as a source, it's identified as `pyup.io-51457`, not as `CVE-2022-42969`, like it is when using `gemnasium`. Thus, if you want to ignore this finding, you will have to run `skjold ignore py CVE-2022-42969` and `skjold ignore py pyup.io-51457` to ignore both of these.

I'm guessing there's just some error (or inconsistency) in how the data from `pyup` is parsed, as the raw data contains the CVE. 

Changing this behaviour can of course break some existing ignore files, but would still be nice if it would work intuitively and following the examples on how to ignore the finding.
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the issue description carefully:

1. The problem is about inconsistent vulnerability identification between two advisory sources: pyup and gemnasium
2. Both sources detect the same vulnerability in the `py==1.11.0` package
3. However, they identify it differently:
   - gemnasium identifies it as `CVE-2022-42969`
   - pyup identifies it as `pyup.io-51457`
4. This inconsistency means users have to ignore the same vulnerability twice using different identifiers
5. The issue suggests that pyup's raw data contains the CVE, so there's likely a parsing inconsistency

Key technical details:
- Command used: `echo "py==1.11.0" | skjold audit -s pyup -s gemnasium -`
- Vulnerability: CVE-2022-42969 (ReDoS in py library)
- Sources: pyup and gemnasium
- Identifiers: CVE-2022-42969 vs pyup.io-51457
- Ignore commands needed: `skjold ignore py CVE-2022-42969` AND `skjold ignore py pyup.io-51457`

The goal is to make the parsing consistent so that both sources use the same identifier (preferably the CVE) when available.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes an **inconsistent vulnerability identification** problem in the skjold tool:

**Core Problem**: Two advisory sources (`pyup` and `gemnasium`) detect the same vulnerability in `py==1.11.0` but assign different identifiers:
- **Gemnasium**: Uses `CVE-2022-42969` 
- **PyUp**: Uses `pyup.io-51457`

**Impact**: Users must run two separate ignore commands to suppress the same vulnerability:
- `skjold ignore py CVE-2022-42969`  
- `skjold ignore py pyup.io-51457`

**Root Cause**: The PyUp data parser likely isn't extracting the CVE identifier even though it's present in the raw data.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Run: `echo "py==1.11.0" | skjold audit -s pyup -s gemnasium -`
2. Observe that the same vulnerability gets different identifiers from different sources
3. Attempt to ignore with one identifier - the other source's finding will still appear

### 1.5 Best Practices for Fix
- Maintain backward compatibility where possible
- Prefer standardized identifiers (CVEs) when available
- Ensure consistent parsing across all advisory sources
- Minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality

    17	
    18	## Introduction
    19	It currently supports fetching advisories from the following sources:
    20	
    21	| Source | Name | Notes |
    22	| ------:|:----:|:------|
    23	| [GitHub Advisory Database](https://github.com/advisories) | `github` | Requires Access Token (See [Github](#github)). |
    24	| [PyUP.io safety-db](https://github.com/pyupio/safety-db) | `pyup` | |
    25	| [GitLab gemnasium-db](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/security-products/gemnasium-db) | `gemnasium` | |
    26	| [PYPA Advisory Database](https://github.com/pypa/advisory-db) | `pypa` | Only supports `ECOSYSTEM`! |
    27	| [OSV.dev Database](https://osv.dev) | `osv` | Only supports `ECOSYSTEM`!<br/> Sends package information to [OSV.dev](https://osv.dev) API. |
    28	
    29	No source is enabled by default! Sources can be enabled by setting `sources` list (see [Configuration](#configuration)). There is (currently) no de-duplication meaning that using too many sources at once will result in _a lot_ of duplicates. `skjold` also requires _all_ dependencies to be passed as it *will not* resolve any dependencies at runtime!
    30	
    31	## Motivation
    32	Skjold was initially created for myself to replace `safety`. ~Which appears to no longer receive monthly updates (see [pyupio/safety-db #2282](https://github.com/pyupio/safety-db/issues/2282))~. I wanted something I can run locally and use for my local or private projects/scripts.
    33	
    34	I currently also use it during CI builds and before deploying/publishing containers or packages.
    35	
    36	## Installation
    37	`skjold` can be installed from either [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/skjold/) or directly from [Github](https://github.com/twu/skjold) using `pip`:
    38	
    39	```sh
    40	pip install skjold                                        # Install from PyPI
    41	pip install git+https://github.com/twu/skjold.git@vX.X.X  # Install from Github
    42	```
    43	
    44	This should provide a script named `skjold` that can then be invoked. See [Usage](#usage).
    45	
    46	## Usage
    47	```sh
    48	$ pip list --format=freeze | skjold -v audit --sources gemnasium -
    49	```
    50	
    51	When running `audit` one can either provide a path to a _frozen_ `requirements.txt`, a `poetry.lock` or a `Pipfile.lock` file. Alternatively, dependencies can also be passed in via `stdin`  (formatted as `package==version`).
    52	
    53	`skjold` will maintain a local cache (under `cache_dir`) that will expire automatically after `cache_expires` has passed. The `cache_dir` and `cache_expires` can be adjusted by setting them in  `tools.skjold` section of the projects `pyproject.toml` (see [Configuration](#configuration) for more details). The `cache_dir`will be created automatically, and by default unless otherwise specified will be located under `$HOME/.skjold/cache`.
    54	
    55	For further options please read `skjold --help` and/or `skjold audit --help`.
    56	
    57	### Examples
    58	
    59	All examples involving `github` assume that `SKJOLD_GITHUB_API_TOKEN` is already set (see [Github](#github)).
    60	
    61	```sh
    62	# Using pip list. Checking against GitHub only.
    63	$ pip list --format=freeze | skjold audit -s github -
    64	
    65	# Be verbose. Read directly from supported formats.
    66	$ skjold -v audit requirements.txt
    67	$ skjold -v audit poetry.lock
    68	$ skjold -v audit Pipenv.lock
    69	
    70	# Specify specify multiple inputs at once.
    71	$ skjold -v audit Pipenv.lock poetry.lock requirements.txt
    72	
    73	# Using poetry.
    74	$ poetry export -f requirements.txt | skjold audit -s github -s gemnasium -s pyup -
    75	
    76	# Using poetry, format output as json and pass it on to jq for additional filtering.
    77	$ poetry export -f requirements.txt | skjold audit -o json -s github - | jq '.[0]'
    78	
    79	# Using Pipenv, checking against Github
    80	$ pipenv run pip list --format=freeze | skjold audit -s github -
    81	
    82	# Checking a single package via stdin against Github and format findings as json.
    83	$ echo "urllib3==1.23" | skjold audit -o json -r -s github -
    84	[
    85	  {
    86	    "severity": "HIGH",
    87	    "name": "urllib3",
    88	    "version": "1.23",
    89	    "versions": "<1.24.2",
    90	    "source": "github",
    91	    "summary": "High severity vulnerability that affects urllib3",
    92	    "references": [
    93	      "https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2019-11324"
    94	    ],
    95	    "url": "https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mh33-7rrq-662w"
    96	  }
    97	]
    98	
    99	# Checking a single package via stdin against Gemnasium and report findings (`-o cli`).
   100	$ echo "urllib3==1.23" | skjold audit -o cli -r -s gemnasium -
   101	
   102	urllib3==1.23 (<=1.24.2) via gemnasium
   103	
   104	CRLF injection. In the urllib3 library for Python, CRLF injection is possible
   105	if the attacker controls the request parameter.
   106	https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2019-11236
   107	--
   108	
   109	urllib3==1.23 (<1.24.2) via gemnasium
   110	
   111	Weak Authentication Caused By Improper Certificate Validation. The urllib3
   112	library for Python mishandles certain cases where the desired set of CA
   113	certificates is different from the OS store of CA certificates, which results
   114	in SSL connections succeeding in situations where a verification failure is the
   115	correct outcome. This is related to use of the `ssl_context`, `ca_certs`, or
   116	`ca_certs_dir` argument.
   117	https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2019-11324
   118	--
   119	
   120	urllib3==1.23 (<1.25.9) via gemnasium
   121	
   122	Injection Vulnerability. urllib3 allows CRLF injection if the attacker controls
   123	the HTTP request method, as demonstrated by inserting `CR` and `LF` control
   124	characters in the first argument of `putrequest()`. NOTE: this is similar to
   125	CVE-2020-26116.
   126	https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2020-26137
   127	--
   128	```
   129	
   130	#### Ignore Findings
   131	
   132	Findings can be ignored either by manually adding an entry using the sources identifier to a file named `.skjoldignore` (See [Example](https://github.com/twu/skjold/blob/master/.skjoldignore)) or by using in the CLI. Below are a few possible usage examples.
   133	
   134	```
   135	# Ignore PYSEC-2020-148 finding from PyPA source until a certain date with a specific reason.
   136	$ skjold ignore urllib3 PYSEC-2020-148 --reason "Very good reason." --expires "2021-01-01T00:00:00+00:00"
   137	Ignore urllib3 in PYSEC-2020-148 until 2021-01-01 00:00:00+00:00?
   138	Very good reason.
   139	--
   140	Add to '.skjoldignore'? [y/N]: y
   141	
   142	# Ignore PYSEC-2020-148 finding from PyPA source for 7 days with "No immediate remediation." reason.
   143	$ skjold ignore urllib3 PYSEC-2020-148
   144	Ignore urllib3 in PYSEC-2020-148 until ...?
   145	No immediate remediation.
   146	--
   147	Add to '.skjoldignore'? [y/N]: y
   148	
   149	# Audit `poetry.lock` using a custom `.skjoldignore` file location via `ENV`...
   150	$ SKJOLD_IGNORE_FILE=<path-to-file> skjold audit -s pyup poetry.lock
   151	
   152	# ... or using -i/--ignore-file
   153	$ skjold audit -s pyup -i <path-to-file> poetry.lock
   154	```
   155	
   156	### Configuration
   157	
   158	`skjold` can read its configuration from the `tools.skjold` section of a projects  `pyproject.toml`. Arguments specified via the command-line should take precedence over any configured or default value.
   159	
   160	```toml
   161	[tool.skjold]
   162	sources = ["github", "pyup", "gemnasium"]  # Sources to check against.
   163	report_only = false                        # Exit with non-zero exit code on findings.
   164	report_format = 'json'                     # Output findings as `json`. Default is 'cli'.
   165	cache_dir = '.skjold_cache'                # Cache location (default: `~/.skjold/cache`).
   166	cache_expires = 86400                      # Cache max. age.
   167	ignore_file = '.skjoldignore'              # Ignorefile location (default `.skjoldignore`).
   168	verbose = true                             # Be verbose.
   169	```
   170	
   171	To take a look at the current configuration / defaults run:
   172	```shell
   173	$ skjold config
   174	sources: ['pyup', 'github', 'gemnasium']
   175	report_only: False
   176	report_format: json
   177	verbose: False
   178	cache_dir: .skjold_cache
   179	cache_expires: 86400
   180	ignore_file = '.skjoldignore'
   181	```
   182	
   183	#### Github
   184	
   185	For the `github` source to work you'll need to provide a Github API Token via an `ENV` variable named `SKJOLD_GITHUB_API_TOKEN`. You can [create a new Github Access Token here](https://github.com/settings/tokens). You *do not* have to give it *any* permissions as it is only required to query the [GitHub GraphQL API v4](https://developer.github.com/v4/) API.
   186	
   187	### Version Control Integration
   188	To use `skjold` with the excellent [pre-commit](https://pre-commit.com/) framework add the following to the projects `.pre-commit-config.yaml` after [installation](https://pre-commit.com/#install).
   189	
   190	```yaml
   191	repos:
   192	  - repo: https://github.com/twu/skjold
   193	    rev: vX.X.X
   194	    hooks:
   195	    - id: skjold
   196	      verbose: true  # Important if used with `report_only`, see below.
   197	```
   198	
   199	After running `pre-commit install` the hook should be good to go. To configure `skjold` in this scenario I recommend adding the entire configuration to the projects `pyproject.toml` instead of manipulating the hook `args`. See this projects [pyproject.toml](./pyproject.toml) for an example.
   200	
   201	> **Important!**: When using `skjold` as a `pre-commit`-hook it only gets triggered if you want to commit changed dependency files (e.g. `Pipenv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `requirements.txt`,...).
   202	> It will not continuously check your dependencies on _every_ commit!
   203	
   204	You could run `pre-commit run skjold --all-files` manually in your workflow/scripts or run `skjold` manually.
   205	If you have a better solution please let me know!
   206	
   207	> **Important!**: If you use `report_only` in any way make sure that you add `verbose: true` to your hook configuration
   208	otherwise `pre-commit` won't show you any output since the hook is always returning with a zero exit code due
   209	to `report_only` being set!
   210

   212	Pull requests are welcome! For major changes, please open an issue first to discuss what you would like to change.
   213	
   214	Please make sure to update tests as appropriate.

py==1.11.0 (<=1.11.0) via gemnasium as CVE-2022-42969 found in <stdin>

Withdrawn Advisory: ReDoS in py library when used with subversion . ###
Withdrawn Advisory This advisory has been withdrawn because evidence does not
suggest that CVE-2022-42969 is a valid, reproducible vulnerability. This link
is maintained to preserve external references.  ### Original Description The py
library through 1.11.0 for Python allows remote attackers to conduct a ReDoS
(Regular expression Denial of Service) attack via a Subversion repository with
crafted info data, because the InfoSvnCommand argument is mishandled.  The
particular codepath in question is the regular expression at
`py._path.svnurl.InfoSvnCommand.lspattern` and is only relevant when dealing
with subversion (svn) projects. Notably the codepath is not used in the popular
pytest project. The developers of the pytest package have released version
`7.2.0` which removes their dependency on `py`. Users of `pytest` seeing alerts
relating to this advisory may update to version `7.2.0` of `pytest` to resolve
this issue. See https://github.com/pytest-
dev/py/issues/287#issuecomment-1290407715 for additional context.
https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2022-42969

    17

    19	    def using(cls, name: str, json_: dict) -> "PyUpSecurityAdvisory":
    20	        obj = cls()
    21	        obj._json = json_
    22	        obj._json["name"] = name
    23	        return obj

    39	        path = self._json.get("more_info_path")
    40	        if not path:
    41	            return ""
    42	        return f"https://pyup.io{path}"

    58	        return self._json["advisory"]

    60	    @property
    61	    def vulnerable_version_range(self) -> List[specifiers.SpecifierSet]:
    62	        return [
    63	            specifiers.SpecifierSet(v, prereleases=True) for v in self._json["specs"]
    64	        ]
    65	
    66	    @property
    67	    def vulnerable_versions(self) -> str:
    68	        return ",".join([str(x) for x in self.vulnerable_version_range])
    69	
    70	    def is_affected(self, version: str) -> bool:
    71	        version_ = specifiers.parse(version)
    72	        allows_: Callable[[specifiers.SpecifierSet], bool] = (
    73	            lambda x: True if version_ in x else False
    74	        )
    75	        # affected_versions = map(lambda x: x.allows(version), self.vulnerable_version_range)
    76	        affected_versions = map(allows_, self.vulnerable_version_range)
    77	        return any(affected_versions)

    80	class PyUp(SecurityAdvisorySource):
    81	
    82	    _url: str = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pyupio/safety-db/master/data/insecure_full.json"
    83	    _name: str = "pyup"
    84	    _metadata: Dict[str, Union[str, int]] = {}
    85	
    86	    @property
    87	    def name(self) -> str:
    88	        return self._name
    89	
    90	    @property
    91	    def total_count(self) -> int:
    92	        return len(self._advisories.keys())
    93	
    94	    @property
    95	    def path(self) -> str:
    96	        return os.path.join(self._cache_dir, "pyup.cache")
    97	
    98	    @property
    99	    def last_updated_at(self) -> datetime.datetime:
   100	        timestamp = int(self._metadata["timestamp"])
   101	        return datetime.datetime.utcfromtimestamp(timestamp)
   102	
   103	    def _load_cache(self) -> Any:
   104	        with open(self.path, "rb") as fh:
   105	            json_ = json.load(fh)
   106	            return json_
   107	
   108	    def populate_from_cache(self) -> None:
   109	        self._advisories = defaultdict(list)
   110	
   111	        cache_ = self._load_cache()
   112	        for package_name, advisories in cache_.items():
   113	            if package_name in {"$meta"}:
   114	                self._metadata = advisories
   115	                continue
   116	
   117	            for advisory in advisories:
   118	                obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using(package_name, advisory)
   119	                self._advisories[obj.canonical_name].append(obj)
   120	
   121	    def update(self) -> None:
   122	        request_ = urllib.request.Request(
   123	            url=self._url,
   124	            headers={"Accept": "application/json"},
   125	        )
   126	        with urllib.request.urlopen(request_) as response:
   127	            json_ = json.loads(response.read())
   128	
   129	        with open(self.path, "w") as fh:
   130	            json.dump(json_, fh)
   131	
   132	    def has_security_advisory_for(self, dependency: Dependency) -> bool:
   133	        return dependency.canonical_name in self.advisories.keys()
   134	
   135	    def is_vulnerable_package(
   136	        self, dependency: Dependency
   137	    ) -> Tuple[bool, List[SecurityAdvisory]]:
   138	        advisories = []
   139	        for candidate in self.advisories[dependency.canonical_name]:
   140	            if candidate.is_affected(dependency.version):
   141	                advisories.append(candidate)
   142	
   143	        return len(advisories) > 0, advisories

    37	
    38	    @property
    39	    def severity(self) -> str:
    40	
    41	        for field in ["cvss_v3", "cvss_v2"]:
    42	            vector = self._json.get(field, None)
    43	            if vector:
    44	                cvss = parse_cvss(vector)
    45	                return cvss.severity
    46	
    47	        return "UNKNOWN"
    48	
    49	    @property
    50	    def url(self) -> str:
    51	        return str(self.references[0])
    52	
    53	    @property
    54	    def references(self) -> List[str]:
    55	        return [str(url) for url in self._json["urls"]]
    56	
    57	    @property
    58	    def package_name(self) -> str:
    59	        return str(self._json["package_slug"]).replace("pypi/", "").strip()
    60	
    61	    @property
    62	    def canonical_name(self) -> NormalizedName:
    63	        return canonicalize_name(self.package_name)
    64	
    65	    @property
    66	    def summary(self) -> str:
    67	        return f"{self._json['title']}. {self._json['description']}"
    68	
    69	    @property
    70	    def vulnerable_version_range(self) -> List[specifiers.SpecifierSet]:
    71	        affected_range = self._json["affected_range"]
    72	
    73	        # Gemnasium seems to invalidate/withdraw advisories by marking them this way.
    74	        # See pypi/pyspark/CVE-2020-27218.yml#L11 in gemnasium-db.
    75	        if affected_range in {"(,0)"}:
    76	            return []
    77	
    78	        if not affected_range:
    79	            return [specifiers.SpecifierSet(">=0.0.0", prereleases=True)]
    80	
    81	        return [
    82	            specifiers.SpecifierSet(x, prereleases=True)
    83	            for x in affected_range.split("||")
    84	        ]
    85	
    86	    @property
    87	    def vulnerable_versions(self) -> str:
    88	        return ",".join([str(x) for x in self.vulnerable_version_range])
    89	
    90	    def is_affected(self, version: str) -> bool:
    91	        version_ = specifiers.parse(version)
    92	        allows_: Callable[[specifiers.SpecifierSet], bool] = (
    93	            lambda x: True if version_ in x else False
    94	        )
    95	        # affected_versions = map(lambda x: x.allows(version), self.vulnerable_version_range)
    96	        affected_versions = map(allows_, self.vulnerable_version_range)
    97	        return any(affected_versions)
    98	
    99	
   100	class Gemnasium(SecurityAdvisorySource):
   101	
   102	    _url = "https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/security-products/gemnasium-db/-/archive/master/gemnasium-db-master.tar.gz"
   103	    _name = "gemnasium"
   104	
   105	    @property
   106	    def name(self) -> str:
   107	        return self._name
   108	
   109	    @property
   110	    def path(self) -> str:
   111	        return os.path.join(self._cache_dir, "gemnasium.cache")
   112	
   113	    def populate_from_cache(self) -> None:
   114	        self._advisories = defaultdict(list)
   115	        with tarfile.TarFile.open(self.path, mode="r:gz") as archive:
   116	            pypi_advisories = filter(
   117	                lambda obj: "/pypi/" in obj.name and obj.name.endswith(".yml"),
   118	                archive.getmembers(),
   119	            )
   120	
   121	            for obj in list(pypi_advisories):
   122	                obj_fh = archive.extractfile(obj.name)
   123	                if obj_fh:
   124	                    doc = yaml.load(obj_fh, Loader=yaml.SafeLoader)
   125	                    advisory = GemnasiumSecurityAdvisory.using(doc)
   126	                    self._advisories[advisory.canonical_name].append(advisory)
   127	                else:  # pragma: no cover
   128	                    raise SkjoldException(
   129	                        f"Unable to extract '{obj.name}' from source archive."
   130	                    )
   131	
   132	    @property
   133	    def total_count(self) -> int:
   134	        return len(self._advisories.keys())
   135	
   136	    def update(self) -> None:
   137	        request = urllib.request.Request(
   138	            url=self._url, headers={"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0"}
   139	        )
   140	        with urllib.request.urlopen(request) as response:
   141	            with open(self.path, "wb") as fh:
   142	                fh.write(response.read())
   143	
   144	    def has_security_advisory_for(self, dependency: Dependency) -> bool:
   145	        return dependency.canonical_name in self.advisories.keys()
   146	
   147	    def is_vulnerable_package(
   148	        self, dependency: Dependency
   149	    ) -> Tuple[bool, List[SecurityAdvisory]]:
   150	        if not self.has_security_advisory_for(dependency):
   151	            return False, []
   152	
   153	        advisories = []
   154	        for candidate in self.advisories[dependency.canonical_name]:
   155	            if candidate.is_affected(dependency.version):
   156	                advisories.append(candidate)
   157	
   158	        return len(advisories) > 0, advisories
   159	
   160	
   161	register_source("gemnasium", Gemnasium)
   162

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Script to reproduce the inconsistent ignoring issue.
This script demonstrates that PyUp and Gemnasium sources use different identifiers
for the same vulnerability, requiring users to ignore both separately.
"""

import subprocess
import tempfile
import os

def run_skjold_audit():
    """Run the skjold audit command that demonstrates the issue."""
    cmd = ['echo', 'py==1.11.0', '|', 'skjold', 'audit', '-s', 'pyup', '-s', 'gemnasium', '-']
    # Join the command properly for shell execution
    full_cmd = ' '.join(['echo "py==1.11.0"', '|', 'skjold', 'audit', '-s', 'pyup', '-s', 'gemnasium', '-'])
    
    print("Running command:", full_cmd)
    print("="*80)
    
    result = subprocess.run(full_cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)
    print(result.stdout)
    if result.stderr:
        print("STDERR:", result.stderr)
    print("="*80)
    print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
    
    return result.stdout

def analyze_identifiers(output):
    """Analyze the output to extract identifiers from both sources."""
    lines = output.split('\n')
    identifiers = {'pyup': [], 'gemnasium': []}
    
    current_source = None
    for line in lines:
        if 'via pyup as' in line:
            # Extract identifier after "as" and before "found"
            parts = line.split('as')
            if len(parts) > 1:
                identifier = parts[1].split('found')[0].strip()
                identifiers['pyup'].append(identifier)
                print(f"PyUp identifier found: {identifier}")
        elif 'via gemnasium as' in line:
            # Extract identifier after "as" and before "found"
            parts = line.split('as')
            if len(parts) > 1:
                identifier = parts[1].split('found')[0].strip()
                identifiers['gemnasium'].append(identifier)
                print(f"Gemnasium identifier found: {identifier}")
    
    return identifiers

def main():
    print("Reproducing the inconsistent ignoring issue...")
    print()
    
    # Run the audit command
    output = run_skjold_audit()
    
    # Analyze identifiers
    identifiers = analyze_identifiers(output)
    
    print("\nSummary:")
    print(f"PyUp identifiers: {identifiers['pyup']}")
    print(f"Gemnasium identifiers: {identifiers['gemnasium']}")
    
    # Check if the issue exists
    if identifiers['pyup'] and identifiers['gemnasium']:
        pyup_id = identifiers['pyup'][0]
        gemnasium_id = identifiers['gemnasium'][0]
        
        if pyup_id != gemnasium_id:
            print(f"\n❌ ISSUE CONFIRMED: Different identifiers for same vulnerability!")
            print(f"   PyUp uses: {pyup_id}")
            print(f"   Gemnasium uses: {gemnasium_id}")
            print(f"   User must run BOTH:")
            print(f"     skjold ignore py {gemnasium_id}")
            print(f"     skjold ignore py {pyup_id}")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"\n✅ No issue found - both sources use the same identifier: {pyup_id}")
            return False
    else:
        print("\n⚠️ Could not extract identifiers from output")
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/reproduce_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/twu__skjold__0.5 && python reproduce_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Reproducing the inconsistent ignoring issue...

Withdrawn Advisory: ReDoS in py library when used with subversion . ###
Withdrawn Advisory This advisory has been withdrawn because evidence does not
suggest that CVE-2022-42969 is a valid, reproducible vulnerability. This link
is maintained to preserve external references.  ### Original Description The py
library through 1.11.0 for Python allows remote attackers to conduct a ReDoS
(Regular expression Denial of Service) attack via a Subversion repository with
crafted info data, because the InfoSvnCommand argument is mishandled.  The
particular codepath in question is the regular expression at
`py._path.svnurl.InfoSvnCommand.lspattern` and is only relevant when dealing
with subversion (svn) projects. Notably the codepath is not used in the popular
pytest project. The developers of the pytest package have released version
`7.2.0` which removes their dependency on `py`. Users of `pytest` seeing alerts
relating to this advisory may update to version `7.2.0` of `pytest` to resolve
this issue. See https://github.com/pytest-
dev/py/issues/287#issuecomment-1290407715 for additional context.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly analyze the problem and solution:

### PROBLEM:
1. **Location**: The issue is in `/workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/src/skjold/sources/pyup.py`, specifically in the `PyUpSecurityAdvisory.identifier` property (lines 25-27).

2. **What happens**: The PyUpSecurityAdvisory class returns `self._json["id"]` as the identifier, which gives identifiers like `pyup.io-51457`. However, the same advisory data also contains a `cve` field with the actual CVE identifier (e.g., `CVE-2022-42969`).

3. **Why it's problematic**: Other sources like Gemnasium use the CVE as the identifier, so users have to ignore the same vulnerability twice with different identifiers.

### SOLUTION:
Modify the `identifier` property in `PyUpSecurityAdvisory` to prefer the CVE when available, falling back to the original ID when no CVE exists.

Looking at the data structure from our investigation:
```json
{
  "id": "pyup.io-51457",
  "cve": "CVE-2022-42969",
  "more_info_path": "/vulnerabilities/CVE-2022-42969/51457"
}
```

The fix should:
1. Check if `self._json.get("cve")` exists and is not null/empty
2. If it exists, return the CVE as the identifier
3. Otherwise, fall back to the original `self._json["id"]`

This approach:
- Maintains backward compatibility (when no CVE is available, old behavior continues)
- Makes ignoring consistent across sources (both will use CVE when available)
- Is minimal and focused

### BEST PRACTICES TO CONSIDER:
1. Handle edge cases: What if cve field exists but is null, empty string, or "null"?
2. Preserve existing behavior for advisories without CVEs
3. Make the change minimal and focused
4. Don't break existing functionality</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The issue is in the `PyUpSecurityAdvisory` class in `/workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/src/skjold/sources/pyup.py`. The `identifier` property only returns `self._json["id"]`, which produces identifiers like `pyup.io-51457`, even though the same advisory data contains the actual CVE identifier in the `cve` field.

### 5.2 Problem Location
File: `/workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/src/skjold/sources/pyup.py`
Method: `PyUpSecurityAdvisory.identifier` (lines 25-27)

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The reproduction script runs `echo "py==1.11.0" | skjold audit -s pyup -s gemnasium -` and shows:
- PyUp identifies the vulnerability as `pyup.io-51457`
- Gemnasium identifies the same vulnerability as `CVE-2022-42969`
- Users must run two separate ignore commands

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
1. **Backward Compatibility**: Fall back to the original ID when no CVE exists
2. **Edge Case Handling**: Properly handle null, empty, or invalid CVE values
3. **Minimal Change**: Focus only on the identifier logic without affecting other functionality
4. **Consistency**: Make behavior consistent with other sources that prefer CVE identifiers

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Modify the `identifier` property to:
1. Check if `self._json.get("cve")` exists and is valid (not null/empty)
2. Return the CVE as identifier when available
3. Fall back to `self._json["id"]` when no valid CVE exists

"""

from skjold.sources.pyup import PyUpSecurityAdvisory

def test_with_cve():
    """Test advisory with CVE - should return CVE as identifier."""
    advisory_data = {
        "id": "pyup.io-51457",
        "cve": "CVE-2022-42969",
        "advisory": "Test advisory",
        "specs": ["<=1.11.0"]
    }
    
    obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using("py", advisory_data)
    identifier = obj.identifier
    print(f"With CVE: {identifier}")
    assert identifier == "CVE-2022-42969", f"Expected CVE-2022-42969, got {identifier}"

def test_without_cve():
    """Test advisory without CVE - should return original ID."""
    advisory_data = {
        "id": "pyup.io-12345",
        "cve": None,
        "advisory": "Test advisory",
        "specs": ["<=1.0.0"]
    }
    
    obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using("somepackage", advisory_data)
    identifier = obj.identifier
    print(f"Without CVE (None): {identifier}")
    assert identifier == "pyup.io-12345", f"Expected pyup.io-12345, got {identifier}"

def test_without_cve_field():
    """Test advisory without CVE field - should return original ID."""
    advisory_data = {
        "id": "pyup.io-67890",
        "advisory": "Test advisory",
        "specs": ["<=2.0.0"]
    }
    
    obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using("anotherpackage", advisory_data)
    identifier = obj.identifier
    print(f"Without CVE field: {identifier}")
    assert identifier == "pyup.io-67890", f"Expected pyup.io-67890, got {identifier}"

    """Test advisory with CVE as 'null' string - should return original ID."""

        "cve": "null",
        "advisory": "Test advisory",
        "specs": ["<=3.0.0"]

    identifier = obj.identifier

    assert identifier == "pyup.io-11111", f"Expected pyup.io-11111, got {identifier}"

def test_with_empty_cve():

            ("pyup", Dependency("does-not-exist", "0"), False),
        ],
    )
    def test_ensure_source_is_affected_single(
        source_name: str,
        dependency: Dependency,
        is_vulnerable: bool,
        cache_dir: str,
    ) -> None:

        from skjold.tasks import _sources

        assert source_name in _sources
        source = _sources[source_name](cache_dir, 3600)
        assert source.name == source_name
        _ = source.advisories
        assert source.total_count > 0

        if is_vulnerable:
            assert source.has_security_advisory_for(dependency)

        vulnerable, _ = source.is_vulnerable_package(dependency)

py==1.11.0 (<=1.11.0) via gemnasium as CVE-2022-42969 found in <stdin>

Withdrawn Advisory: ReDoS in py library when used with subversion . ###
Withdrawn Advisory This advisory has been withdrawn because evidence does not
suggest that CVE-2022-42969 is a valid, reproducible vulnerability. This link
is maintained to preserve external references.  ### Original Description The py
library through 1.11.0 for Python allows remote attackers to conduct a ReDoS
(Regular expression Denial of Service) attack via a Subversion repository with
crafted info data, because the InfoSvnCommand argument is mishandled.  The
particular codepath in question is the regular expression at
`py._path.svnurl.InfoSvnCommand.lspattern` and is only relevant when dealing
with subversion (svn) projects. Notably the codepath is not used in the popular
pytest project. The developers of the pytest package have released version
`7.2.0` which removes their dependency on `py`. Users of `pytest` seeing alerts
relating to this advisory may update to version `7.2.0` of `pytest` to resolve
this issue. See https://github.com/pytest-
dev/py/issues/287#issuecomment-1290407715 for additional context.
https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2022-42969

Urllib3 1.26.17 and 2.0.5 include a fix for CVE-2023-43804: Urllib3 doesn't
treat the 'Cookie' HTTP header special or provide any helpers for managing
cookies over HTTP, that is the responsibility of the user. However, it is
possible for a user to specify a 'Cookie' header and unknowingly leak
information via HTTP redirects to a different origin if that user doesn't

urllib3==1.23 (<1.26.18,<2.0.7,>=2.0.0a1) via pyup as CVE-2023-45803 found in <stdin>

Affected versions of urllib3 are vulnerable to an HTTP redirect handling
vulnerability that fails to remove the HTTP request body when a POST changes to
a GET via 301, 302, or 303 responses. This flaw can expose sensitive request
data if the origin service is compromised and redirects to a malicious
endpoint, though exploitability is low when no sensitive data is used. The
vulnerability affects automatic redirect behavior. It is fixed in versions
1.26.18 and 2.0.7; update or disable redirects using redirects=False.  This
vulnerability is specific to Python's urllib3 library.
https://pyup.io/vulnerabilities/CVE-2023-45803/61893

containing many @ characters in the authority component, the authority regular
expression exhibits catastrophic backtracking, causing a denial of service if a

urllib3==1.23 (<2.5.0) via pyup as CVE-2025-50181 found in <stdin>

urllib3 is a user-friendly HTTP client library for Python. Prior to 2.5.0, it
is possible to disable redirects for all requests by instantiating a
PoolManager and specifying retries in a way that disable redirects. By default,
requests and botocore users are not affected. An application attempting to
mitigate SSRF or open redirect vulnerabilities by disabling redirects at the
PoolManager level will remain vulnerable. This issue has been patched in
version 2.5.0.
https://pyup.io/vulnerabilities/CVE-2025-50181/77744

--

urllib3==1.23 (<2.5.0) via pyup as CVE-2025-50182 found in <stdin>

Urllib3 is a user-friendly HTTP client library for Python. Starting in version
2.2.0 and before 2.5.0, urllib3 does not control redirects in browsers and
Node.js. urllib3 supports being used in a Pyodide runtime, utilizing the
JavaScript Fetch API or falling back on XMLHttpRequest. This means Python
libraries can be used to make HTTP requests from a browser or Node.js.
Additionally, urllib3 provides a mechanism to control redirects, but the
retries and redirect parameters are ignored with Pyodide; the runtime itself
determines redirect behaviour. This issue has been patched in version 2.5.0.
https://pyup.io/vulnerabilities/CVE-2025-50182/77745

--

urllib3==1.23 (<=1.26.18,<=2.2.1,>=2.0.0a1) via pyup as CVE-2024-37891 found in <stdin>

Urllib3's ProxyManager ensures that the Proxy-Authorization header is correctly
directed only to configured proxies. However, when HTTP requests bypass
urllib3's proxy support, there's a risk of inadvertently setting the Proxy-
Authorization header, which remains ineffective without a forwarding or
tunneling proxy. Urllib3 does not recognize this header as carrying
authentication data, failing to remove it during cross-origin redirects. While
this scenario is uncommon and poses low risk to most users, urllib3 now
proactively removes the Proxy-Authorization header during cross-origin
redirects as a precautionary measure. Users are advised to utilize urllib3's
proxy support or disable automatic redirects to handle the Proxy-Authorization
header securely. Despite these precautions, urllib3 defaults to stripping the
header to safeguard users who may inadvertently misconfigure requests.
https://pyup.io/vulnerabilities/CVE-2024-37891/71608

Let me create a final comprehensive test to demonstrate that the fix works:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/twu__skjold__0.5/final_verification.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Final verification that the fix for inconsistent ignoring works correctly.
"""

import subprocess
import tempfile
import os

def test_original_issue_fixed():
    """Test that the original issue is now fixed."""
    print("🔍 Testing the original issue fix...")
    
    # Run the command from the issue description
    cmd = 'echo "py==1.11.0" | skjold audit -s pyup -s gemnasium -'
    result = subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)
    
    # Parse the output to check identifiers
    output_lines = result.stdout.split('\n')
    identifiers = []
    
    for line in output_lines:
        if 'via pyup as' in line and 'found in' in line:
            start_pos = line.find('via pyup as') + len('via pyup as')
            end_pos = line.find('found in')
            if start_pos > -1 and end_pos > start_pos:
                identifier = line[start_pos:end_pos].strip()
                identifiers.append(('pyup', identifier))
        elif 'via gemnasium as' in line and 'found in' in line:
            start_pos = line.find('via gemnasium as') + len('via gemnasium as')
            end_pos = line.find('found in')
            if start_pos > -1 and end_pos > start_pos:
                identifier = line[start_pos:end_pos].strip()
                identifiers.append(('gemnasium', identifier))
    
    print(f"   Found identifiers: {identifiers}")
    
    # Check that both sources now use the same identifier
    pyup_ids = [id for source, id in identifiers if source == 'pyup']
    gemnasium_ids = [id for source, id in identifiers if source == 'gemnasium']
    
    if pyup_ids and gemnasium_ids and pyup_ids[0] == gemnasium_ids[0]:
        print(f"   ✅ SUCCESS: Both sources now use the same identifier: {pyup_ids[0]}")
        print(f"   🎉 Users now only need to run: skjold ignore py {pyup_ids[0]}")
        return True
    else:
        print(f"   ❌ FAILED: Sources still use different identifiers")
        print(f"   PyUp identifiers: {pyup_ids}")
        print(f"   Gemnasium identifiers: {gemnasium_ids}")
        return False

def test_backward_compatibility():
    """Test that advisories without CVEs still work."""
    print("\n🔍 Testing backward compatibility...")
    
    from skjold.sources.pyup import PyUpSecurityAdvisory
    
    # Test advisory without CVE
    advisory_data_no_cve = {
        "id": "pyup.io-12345",
        "advisory": "Test advisory without CVE",
        "specs": ["<1.0.0"]
    }
    
    obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using("testpackage", advisory_data_no_cve)
    identifier = obj.identifier
    
    if identifier == "pyup.io-12345":
        print(f"   ✅ SUCCESS: Advisory without CVE uses original ID: {identifier}")
        return True
    else:
        print(f"   ❌ FAILED: Expected 'pyup.io-12345', got '{identifier}'")
        return False

def test_cve_preferred():
    """Test that CVE is preferred when available.""" 
    print("\n🔍 Testing CVE preference...")
    
    from skjold.sources.pyup import PyUpSecurityAdvisory
    
    # Test advisory with CVE
    advisory_data_with_cve = {
        "id": "pyup.io-51457", 
        "cve": "CVE-2022-42969",
        "advisory": "Test advisory with CVE",
        "specs": ["<=1.11.0"]
    }
    
    obj = PyUpSecurityAdvisory.using("py", advisory_data_with_cve)
    identifier = obj.identifier
    
    if identifier == "CVE-2022-42969":
        print(f"   ✅ SUCCESS: Advisory with CVE uses CVE identifier: {identifier}")
        return True
    else:
        print(f"   ❌ FAILED: Expected 'CVE-2022-42969', got '{identifier}'")
        return False

def main():
    print("🧪 Final Verification of Inconsistent Ignoring Fix")
    print("=" * 60)
    
    # Run all tests
    test1_passed = test_original_issue_fixed()
    test2_passed = test_backward_compatibility() 
    test3_passed = test_cve_preferred()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    if test1_passed and test2_passed and test3_passed:
        print("🎉 ALL TESTS PASSED! The fix is working correctly.")
        print("\n📋 Summary of changes:")
        print("   • PyUp now prefers CVE identifiers when available")
        print("   • Consistent with Gemnasium and other sources") 
        print("   • Backward compatible with advisories without CVEs")
        print("   • Users only need one ignore command instead of two")
        return True
    else:
        print("❌ Some tests failed. Please review the implementation.")
        return False
